+289|    def _load_allowances(self):
+290|        lines = content_lines(self.p("site_allowances.tsv"))
+291|        self.counts["site_allowances.tsv"] = max(0, len(lines) - 1)
+292|        idx = {name: i for i, name in enumerate(lines[0].split("\t"))}
+293|        i_site, i_ticks, i_from = idx["site_id"], idx["allowance_ticks"], 
idx["draws_from"]
+294|        self.allowance_ticks = {}
+295|        draws = {}
+296|        for l in lines[1:]:
+297|            f = l.split("\t")
+298|            site_id = f[i_site]
+299|            self.allowance_ticks[site_id] = int(f[i_ticks])
+300|            draws[site_id] = f[i_from].strip() if i_from < len(f) else ""
+301|        root = {}
+302|        for site_id in self.allowance_ticks:
+303|            chain = []
+304|            cur = site_id
+305|            while cur not in root and draws.get(cur):
+306|                chain.append(cur)
+307|                cur = draws[cur]
+308|            cur = root.get(cur, cur)
+309|            for s in chain:
+310|                root[s] = cur
+311|            root[site_id] = cur
+312|        self.allowance_root = root
+313|
+314|
+315|def build_offers(ex):
+316|    """One Offer per non-orphan offer, with interval ticks, totals and 
credit."""
+317|    out = []
+318|    den = 1000 * ex.millicents_per_cent
+319|    wpt = ex.wh_per_tick
+320|    for r in ex.offer_rows:
+321|        sr = ex.site_rows[(r["event_id"], r["site_id"])]
+322|        n = ex.events[r["event_id"]]["interval_count"]
+323|        prof = ex.profiles.get(r["offer_id"], {})
+324|        rate = sr["rate"]
+325|        rel, rec, credit = [], [], 0
+326|        for t in range(1, n + 1):
+327|            a, b = prof.get(t, (0, 0))
+328|            rel.append(a)
+329|            rec.append(b)
+330|            if a > b:
+331|                credit += rhe((a - b) * wpt * rate, den)
+332|        o = Offer()
+333|        o.oid = r["offer_id"]
+334|        o.event_id = r["event_id"]
+335|        o.site_id = r["site_id"]
+336|        o.bid = int(r["bid_cost_cents"])
+337|        o.segment = sr["segment"]
+338|        o.cap_binding = sr["cap_binding"]
+339|        o.rel = rel
+340|        o.rec = rec
+341|        o.trel = sum(rel)
+342|        o.trec = sum(rec)
+343|        o.credit = credit
+344|        out.append(o)
+345|    return out
+346|
+347|
+348|def solve(ex, event_id, region, site_options):
+349|    """Best feasible portfolio of an event-region; returns (offers, 
tied_count)."""
+350|    ev = ex.events[event_id]
+351|    n = ev["interval_count"]
+352|    target, weight, rel_ceil, rec_ceil = [], [], [], []
+353|    for t in range(1, n + 1):
+354|        tg, w = ex.targets[(event_id, region, t)]
+355|        target.append(tg)
+356|        weight.append(w)
+357|        rc, qc = ex.ceilings[(event_id, region, t)]
+358|        rel_ceil.append(rc)
+359|        rec_ceil.append(qc)
+360|    floors = []
+361|    for seg in ex.segments:
+362|        f, pw = ex.floors.get((event_id, region, seg), (0, 0))
+363|        if f > 0 and pw > 0:
+364|            floors.append((seg, f, pw))
+365|    seg_index = {seg: i for i, (seg, _f, _w) in enumerate(floors)}
+366|    nseg = len(floors)
+367|    cost_weight = ev["cost_weight"]
+368|    recovery_weight = ev["recovery_weight"]
+369|
+370|    options = [[None] + sorted(site_options[s], key=lambda o: o.oid)
+371|               for s in sorted(site_options)]
+372|    depth = len(options)
+373|
+374|    rel = [0] * n
+375|    rec = [0] * n
+376|    segrel = [0] * nseg
+377|    chosen = []
+378|    acc = [0, 0]  # bid total, recovery tick total
+379|    best = [None, None, 0]
+380|
+381|    def evaluate():
+382|        shortfall = 0
+383|        over = 0
+384|        for t in range(n):
+385|            d = target[t] - rel[t]
+386|            if d > 0:
+387|                shortfall += weight[t] * d
+388|            elif d < 0:
+389|                over -= d
+390|        deficit = 0
+391|        for i in range(nseg):
+392|            d = floors[i][1] - segrel[i]
+393|            if d > 0:
+394|                deficit += floors[i][2] * d
+395|        key = (shortfall, deficit, cost_weight * acc[0] + recovery_weight *
acc[1],
+396|               over, len(chosen))
+397|        if best[0] is None or key < best[0]:
+398|            best[0] = key
+399|            best[1] = tuple(sorted(o.oid for o in chosen))
+400|            best[2] = 1
+401|        elif key == best[0]:
+402|            best[2] += 1
+403|            ids = tuple(sorted(o.oid for o in chosen))
+404|            if ids > best[1]:
+405|                best[1] = ids
+406|
+407|    def dfs(i):
+408|        if i == depth:
+409|            evaluate()
+410|            return
+411|        for o in options[i]:
+412|            if o is None:
+413|                dfs(i + 1)
+414|                continue
+415|            orel, orec = o.rel, o.rec
+416|            feasible = True
+417|            for t in range(n):
+418|                if rel[t] + orel[t] > rel_ceil[t] or rec[t] + orec[t] > 
rec_ceil[t]:
+419|                    feasible = False
+420|                    break
+421|            if not feasible:
+422|                continue
+423|            for t in range(n):
+424|                rel[t] += orel[t]
+425|                rec[t] += orec[t]
+426|            si = seg_index.get(o.segment)
+427|            if si is not None:
+428|                segrel[si] += o.trel
+429|            acc[0] += o.bid
+430|            acc[1] += o.trec
+431|            chosen.append(o)
+432|            dfs(i + 1)
+433|            chosen.pop()
+434|            acc[0] -= o.bid
+435|            acc[1] -= o.trec
+436|            if si is not None:
+437|                segrel[si] -= o.trel
+438|            for t in range(n):
+439|                rel[t] -= orel[t]
+440|                rec[t] -= orec[t]
+441|
+442|    dfs(0)
+443|    by_id = {o.oid: o for opts in options for o in opts if o is not None}
+444|    return [by_id[i] for i in best[1]], best[2]
+445|
+446|
+447|def largest_remainder(F, weights, order_desc):
+448|    """Apportion F by weight using largest remainder.
+449|
+450|    order_desc lists the beneficiaries in descending identifier order, 
which is how
+451|    equal remainders are served.  Returns (shares, residual cents, residual
tie).
+452|    """
+453|    W = sum(weights.values())
+454|    shares = {b: F * w // W for b, w in weights.items()}
+455|    rem = {b: (F * w) % W for b, w in weights.items()}
+456|    left = F - sum(shares.values())
+457|    seen = set()
+458|    tie = False
+459|    for r in rem.values():
+460|        if r:
+461|            if r in seen:
+462|                tie = True
+463|            seen.add(r)
+464|    order = list(order_desc)
+465|    order.sort(key=lambda b: -rem[b])
+466|    for b in order[:left]:
+467|        shares[b] += 1
+468|    return shares, left, tie
+469|
+470|
+471|def compute_grants(ex, pool, carried, credit_cents, site_rows):
+472|    """Fund and apportion one event-region's grant pool."""
+473|    F = pool + carried - credit_cents
+474|    if F < 0:
+475|        F = 0
+476|    weights = {}
+477|    recipients = 0
+478|    for sr in site_rows:
+479|        if "grant_weight" not in sr:
+480|            continue
+481|        recipients += 1
+482|        w = sr["grant_weight"]
+483|        if sr["label"] == "CONTINUITY_WAIVED":
+484|            w *= 2
+485|        b = sr["beneficiary_id"]
+486|        weights[b] = weights.get(b, 0) + w
+487|    W = sum(weights.values())
+488|    out = {"pool": pool, "F": F, "allocated": 0, "unallocated": F, 
"grants": [],
+489|           "residual": 0, "residual_tie": False,
+490|           "zero_denominator": recipients == 0 or W == 0}
+491|    if out["zero_denominator"]:
+492|        return out
+493|
+494|    positive = {b: w for b, w in weights.items() if w > 0}
+495|    order_desc = sorted(positive, reverse=True)
+496|    claims = {b: w * ex.claim_cents_per_weight for b, w in 
positive.items()}
+497|    if sum(claims.values()) <= F:
+498|        shares, residual, tie = claims, 0, False
+499|    else:
+500|        shares, residual, tie = largest_remainder(F, positive, order_desc)
+501|        kept = {b: w for b, w in positive.items() if shares[b] >= 
ex.minimum_grant_cents}
+502|        if len(kept) != len(positive):
+503|            if kept:
+504|                second, residual, tie = largest_remainder(
+505|                    F, kept, [b for b in order_desc if b in kept])
+506|                shares = {b: second.get(b, 0) for b in positive}
+507|            else:
+508|                shares, residual, tie = {b: 0 for b in positive}, 0, False
+509|    allocated = sum(shares.values())
+510|    out["allocated"] = allocated
+511|    out["unallocated"] = F - allocated
+512|    out["grants"] = [{"beneficiary_id": b, "cents": shares[b]} for b in 
sorted(positive)]
+513|    out["residual"] = residual
+514|    out["residual_tie"] = tie
+515|    return out
+516|
+517|
+518|def run(ex):
+519|    """Walk the season in order, choosing portfolios and settling 
grants."""
+520|    offers = build_offers(ex)
+521|    by_event_region = {}
+522|    for o in offers:
+523|        key = (o.event_id, ex.site_rows[(o.event_id, o.site_id)]["region"])
+524|        by_event_region.setdefault(key, []).append(o)
+525|    rows_by_event_region = {}
+526|    for sr in ex.site_rows.values():
+527|        rows_by_event_region.setdefault((sr["event_id"], sr["region"]), 
[]).append(sr)
+528|
+529|    remaining = {r: ex.allowance_ticks[r] for r in 
set(ex.allowance_root.values())}
+530|    carried = {r: 0 for r in ex.regions}
+531|    stats = {"ineligible": 0, "blocked": 0, "empty_groups": 0, 
"cap_binding": 0,
+532|             "residual": 0, "residual_tie_pools": 0, "unallocated_pools": 
0}
+533|    results = {}
+534|
+535|    for event_id in ex.season_order:
+536|        n = ex.events[event_id]["interval_count"]
+537|        consumed = []
+538|        for region in ex.regions:
+539|            key = (event_id, region)
+540|            site_options = {}
+541|            for o in by_event_region.get(key, []):
+542|                sr = ex.site_rows[(o.event_id, o.site_id)]
+543|                if not sr.get("dispatch_authorized"):
+544|                    stats["ineligible"] += 1
+545|                    continue
+546|                if o.trel > remaining.get(ex.allowance_root.get(o.site_id),
0):
+547|                    stats["blocked"] += 1
+548|                    continue
+549|                site_options.setdefault(o.site_id, []).append(o)
+550|            candidate_count = sum(len(v) for v in site_options.values())
+551|            if candidate_count == 0:
+552|                stats["empty_groups"] += 1
+553|            chosen, tied = solve(ex, event_id, region, site_options)
+554|            consumed.extend(chosen)
+555|
+556|            rel = [0] * n
+557|            rec = [0] * n
+558|            bid = credit = 0
+559|            for o in chosen:
+560|                for t in range(n):
+561|                    rel[t] += o.rel[t]
+562|                    rec[t] += o.rec[t]
+563|                bid += o.bid
+564|                credit += o.credit
+565|                if o.cap_binding:
+566|                    stats["cap_binding"] += 1
+567|
+568|            site_rows = rows_by_event_region.get(key, ())
+569|            g = compute_grants(ex, ex.pools.get(key, 0), carried[region], 
credit, site_rows)
+570|            carried[region] = g["unallocated"]
+571|            stats["residual"] += g["residual"]
+572|            stats["residual_tie_pools"] += 1 if g["residual_tie"] else 0
+573|            stats["unallocated_pools"] += 1 if g["zero_denominator"] else 0
+574|
+575|            counts = {lab: 0 for lab in LABELS}
+576|            for sr in site_rows:
+577|                counts[sr["label"]] += 1
+578|
+579|            results[key] = {
+580|                "candidate_offer_count": candidate_count,
+581|                "chosen": chosen,
+582|                "rel": rel,
+583|                "rec": rec,
+584|                "bid": bid,
+585|                "credit": credit,
+586|                "grants": g,
+587|                "compliance_counts": counts,
+588|                "tied": tied,
+589|            }
+590|        for o in consumed:
+591|            root = ex.allowance_root.get(o.site_id)
+592|            if root in remaining:
+593|                remaining[root] -= o.trel
+594|    return results, stats
+595|
+596|
+597|REGION_TOTAL_KEYS = ("event_count", "candidate_offer_count", 
"selected_offer_count",
+598|                     "relief_ticks", "recovery_ticks", "shortfall_ticks",
+599|                     "overdelivery_ticks", "bid_cost_cents", 
"credit_cents",
+600|                     "grant_pool_cents", "grant_funded_cents", 
"grant_allocated_cents",
+601|                     "grant_unallocated_cents")
+602|
+603|
+604|def region_doc(ex, region, results):
+605|    events_out = []
+606|    totals = {k: 0 for k in REGION_TOTAL_KEYS}
+607|    for event_id in ex.event_ids:
+608|        r = results[(event_id, region)]
+609|        intervals = []
+610|        rel_sum = rec_sum = short_sum = over_sum = 0
+611|        for t in range(1, ex.events[event_id]["interval_count"] + 1):
+612|            tg = ex.targets[(event_id, region, t)][0]
+613|            rl = r["rel"][t - 1]
+614|            rc = r["rec"][t - 1]
+615|            short = tg - rl if tg > rl else 0
+616|            over = rl - tg if rl > tg else 0
+617|            intervals.append({"interval": t, "target_ticks": tg, 
"relief_ticks": rl,
+618|                              "recovery_ticks": rc, "shortfall_ticks": 
short,
+619|                              "overdelivery_ticks": over})
+620|            rel_sum += rl
+621|            rec_sum += rc
+622|            short_sum += short
+623|            over_sum += over
+624|        g = r["grants"]
+625|        events_out.append({
+626|            "event_id": event_id,
+627|            "candidate_offer_count": r["candidate_offer_count"],
+628|            "selected_offer_ids": sorted(o.oid for o in r["chosen"]),
+629|            "intervals": intervals,
+630|            "bid_cost_cents": r["bid"],
+631|            "credit_cents": r["credit"],
+632|            "grant_pool_cents": g["pool"],
+633|            "grant_funded_cents": g["F"],
+634|            "grant_allocated_cents": g["allocated"],
+635|            "grant_unallocated_cents": g["unallocated"],
+636|            "grants": g["grants"],
+637|            "compliance_counts": r["compliance_counts"],
+638|        })
+639|        totals["event_count"] += 1
+640|        totals["candidate_offer_count"] += r["candidate_offer_count"]
+641|        totals["selected_offer_count"] += len(r["chosen"])
+642|        totals["relief_ticks"] += rel_sum
+643|        totals["recovery_ticks"] += rec_sum
+644|        totals["shortfall_ticks"] += short_sum
+645|        totals["overdelivery_ticks"] += over_sum
+646|        totals["bid_cost_cents"] += r["bid"]
+647|        totals["credit_cents"] += r["credit"]
+648|        totals["grant_pool_cents"] += g["pool"]
+649|        totals["grant_funded_cents"] += g["F"]
+650|        totals["grant_allocated_cents"] += g["allocated"]
+651|        totals["grant_unallocated_cents"] += g["unallocated"]
+652|    return {"region": region, "events": events_out, "totals": totals}
+653|
+654|
+655|def segment_doc(ex, segment, results):
+656|    entries = []
+657|    totals = {k: 0 for k in ("selected_offer_count", "relief_ticks", 
"recovery_ticks",
+658|                             "bid_cost_cents", "credit_cents", 
"floor_ticks")}
+659|    for event_id in ex.event_ids:
+660|        for region in ex.regions:
+661|            chosen = [o for o in results[(event_id, region)]["chosen"]
+662|                      if o.segment == segment]
+663|            rel = sum(o.trel for o in chosen)
+664|            rec = sum(o.trec for o in chosen)
+665|            bid = sum(o.bid for o in chosen)
+666|            credit = sum(o.credit for o in chosen)
+667|            floor = ex.floors.get((event_id, region, segment), (0, 0))[0]
+668|            bp = 10000 if floor == 0 else min(10000, rhu(10000 * rel, 
floor))
+669|            entries.append({
+670|                "event_id": event_id, "region": region,
+671|                "selected_offer_ids": sorted(o.oid for o in chosen),
+672|                "relief_ticks": rel, "recovery_ticks": rec,
+673|                "bid_cost_cents": bid, "credit_cents": credit,
+674|                "floor_ticks": floor, "floor_attainment_bp": bp,
+675|            })
+676|            totals["selected_offer_count"] += len(chosen)
+677|            totals["relief_ticks"] += rel
+678|            totals["recovery_ticks"] += rec
+679|            totals["bid_cost_cents"] += bid
+680|            totals["credit_cents"] += credit
+681|            totals["floor_ticks"] += floor
+682|    return {"segment": segment, "event_regions": entries, "totals": totals}
+683|
+684|
+685|def summary_doc(ex, region_docs, segment_docs):
+686|    keys = [k for k in REGION_TOTAL_KEYS if k != "event_count"]
+687|    doc = {k: 0 for k in keys}
+688|    for region in ex.regions:
+689|        t = region_docs[region]["totals"]
+690|        for k in keys:
+691|            doc[k] += t[k]
+692|    doc["event_count"] = len(ex.event_ids)
+693|    doc["event_region_count"] = len(ex.event_ids) * len(ex.regions)
+694|    doc["site_count"] = len(ex.site_rows)
+695|    doc["regions"] = {
+696|        region: {k: region_docs[region]["totals"][k] for k in
+697|                 ("selected_offer_count", "relief_ticks", "bid_cost_cents",
+698|                  "credit_cents", "grant_allocated_cents")}
+699|        for region in ex.regions}
+700|    doc["segments"] = {
+701|        segment: {k: segment_docs[segment]["totals"][k] for k in
+702|                  ("selected_offer_count", "relief_ticks", 
"bid_cost_cents",
+703|                   "credit_cents")}
+704|        for segment in ex.segments}
+705|    return doc
+706|
+707|
+708|def policy_doc(ex, results, stats):
+709|    counts = {lab: 0 for lab in LABELS}
+710|    labels = []
+711|    for key in sorted(ex.site_rows):
+712|        sr = ex.site_rows[key]
+713|        counts[sr["label"]] += 1
+714|        labels.append({"event_id": sr["event_id"], "site_id": 
sr["site_id"],
+715|                       "label": sr["label"]})
+716|    ties = []
+717|    for event_id in ex.event_ids:
+718|        for region in ex.regions:
+719|            r = results[(event_id, region)]
+720|            if r["tied"] >= 2:
+721|                ties.append({"event_id": event_id, "region": region,
+722|                             "tied_portfolio_count": r["tied"],
+723|                             "selected_offer_ids": sorted(o.oid for o in 
r["chosen"])})
+724|    return {
+725|        "compliance_counts": counts,
+726|        "site_labels": labels,
+727|        "tie_decisions": ties,
+728|        "cap_binding_count": stats["cap_binding"],
+729|        "residual_cents": stats["residual"],
+730|        "residual_tie_pool_count": stats["residual_tie_pools"],
+731|    }
+732|
+733|
+734|def quality_doc(ex, stats):
+735|    zero_floor = 0
+736|    for event_id in ex.event_ids:
+737|        for region in ex.regions:
+738|            for segment in ex.segments:
+739|                if ex.floors.get((event_id, region, segment), (0, 0))[0] ==
0:
+740|                    zero_floor += 1
+741|    return {
+742|        "rows": dict(ex.counts),
+743|        "orphan_offer_rows": ex.orphan_offer_rows,
+744|        "orphan_profile_rows": ex.orphan_profile_rows,
+745|        "out_of_range_profile_rows": ex.out_of_range_profile_rows,
+746|        "duplicate_profile_rows": ex.duplicate_profile_rows,
+747|        "unknown_region_site_rows": ex.unknown_region_site_rows,
+748|        "waiver_signed_missing": ex.waiver_signed_missing,
+749|        "ineligible_offer_count": stats["ineligible"],
+750|        "allowance_blocked_offer_count": stats["blocked"],
+751|        "empty_candidate_groups": stats["empty_groups"],
+752|        "zero_floor_count": zero_floor,
+753|        "unallocated_pool_count": stats["unallocated_pools"],
+754|    }
+755|
+756|
+757|def write_json(path, doc):
+758|    with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
+759|        json.dump(doc, fh, sort_keys=True, indent=1)
+760|        fh.write("\n")
+761|
+762|
+763|def main(argv):
+764|    if len(argv) != 3:
+765|        print("usage: derive_report.py DATA_DIR OUT_DIR", file=sys.stderr)
+766|        return 2
+767|    data_dir, out_dir = argv[1], argv[2]
+768|    os.makedirs(out_dir, exist_ok=True)
+769|    ex = Extract(data_dir)
+770|    results, stats = run(ex)
+771|    region_docs = {r: region_doc(ex, r, results) for r in ex.regions}
+772|    segment_docs = {s: segment_doc(ex, s, results) for s in ex.segments}
+773|    for region, doc in region_docs.items():
+774|        write_json(os.path.join(out_dir, "region_%s.json" % region), doc)
+775|    for segment, doc in segment_docs.items():
+776|        write_json(os.path.join(out_dir, "segment_%s.json" % segment), doc)
+777|    write_json(os.path.join(out_dir, "summary.json"),
+778|               summary_doc(ex, region_docs, segment_docs))
+779|    write_json(os.path.join(out_dir, "policy_audit.json"), policy_doc(ex, 
results, stats))
+780|    write_json(os.path.join(out_dir, "data_quality.json"), quality_doc(ex, 
stats))
+781|    return 0
+782|
+783|
+784|if __name__ == "__main__":
+785|    sys.exit(main(sys.argv))

Agent Action ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Summary: Check profile row anomalies and objective weights

Reasoning:
That grant_funded_cents figure is way larger than the pools themselves, which is
a red flag — unallocated funds carry forward and compound across events, so each
event's total grows by the pool amount since allocated spending is tiny relative
to it, snowballing over the 500 events.

This matches the carry rule as written, and since claims almost never exceed 
this ballooning F, pools always take the claims branch rather than needing 
largest-remainder allocation — which would explain why residual_cents and tie 
counts come out at zero. That's suspicious given how much emphasis the spec 
places on remainder logic, so I'm second-guessing whether I've interpreted the 
carry rule correctly.

Though this could still be relevant spec for other extracts where it matters. 
Checking the credit magnitude: total credit_cents over 4152 offers averages 
around 472 cents each, which lines up with relief ticks converted to Wh and 
priced at the going rate—roughly 5560 ticks per offer translating to about 
389-472 cents. So pools (~104k avg) dwarf credits (~472), confirming credits are
a minor adjustment relative to the pool size.

Verifying the grant_funded_cents total of 51.6 billion: with average F around 
25.8M and summing pool contributions across 500 events per region times four 
regions, I get roughly 52 billion, which matches well. So the carry-cascade 
interpretation is internally consistent.

Now I'm noticing tie_decisions equals 0, which seems suspicious given what the 
spec describes.

For events like W26-0041 where cost_weight and recovery_weight are both zero, 
the blended component always evaluates to 0, making ties between portfolios 
quite plausible on the remaining components. Yet the reported tie_decisions is 
zero, so I want to dig into exactly how many events share this zero-weight 
pattern and whether ties are actually occurring there.

I'm also second-guessing my tie-counting logic — the comparison resetting the 
count when a strictly smaller key appears and incrementing on equality looks 
correct, but I want to verify by printing the distribution of tie counts 
directly rather than trusting the summary stat. I'm also checking other 
suspicious zero-counts: empty_candidate_groups being zero seems plausible given 
every event-region had multiple sites, and out_of_range/duplicate profile rows 
being zero needs confirming against the interval_count data.

Interval counts are supposed to be 6 for every event with values 1 through 6, so
having both out_of_range and duplicate counts come back zero seems too clean 
given how detailed the schema is — I want to directly inspect the interval 
values in profiles.tsv. Orphan profile rows came back as 36, which at least is 
nonzero, so now I'm digging into what kinds of profile rows exist and how the 
ties break down there.

Summary: Brute-force cross-check all portfolio selections

Reasoning:

$ cd /app && python3 - <<'EOF'
import sys, itertools, collections
sys.path.insert(0,'out')
import derive_report as d
ex=d.Extract('data')
offers=d.build_offers(ex)
ber=collections.defaultdict(list)
for o in offers:
    ber[(o.event_id, ex.site_rows[(o.event_id,o.site_id)]['region'])].append(o)

# replicate run() to get the candidate sets it used, then brute force 
independently
res,stats=d.run(ex)

remaining={r:ex.allowance_ticks[r] for r in set(ex.allowance_root.values())}
mismatch=0; checked=0
for event_id in ex.season_order:
    ev=ex.events[event_id]; n=ev['interval_count']
    consumed=[]
    for region in ex.regions:
        cands=[o for o in ber[(event_id,region)]
               if 
ex.site_rows[(o.event_id,o.site_id)].get('dispatch_authorized')
               and o.trel<=remaining.get(ex.allowance_root.get(o.site_id),0)]
        bysite=collections.defaultdict(list)
        for o in cands: bysite[o.site_id].append(o)
        opts=[[None]+sorted(v,key=lambda o:o.oid) for k,v in 
sorted(bysite.items())]
        tg=[ex.targets[(event_id,region,t)] for t in range(1,n+1)]
        ce=[ex.ceilings[(event_id,region,t)] for t in range(1,n+1)]
        fl={s:ex.floors.get((event_id,region,s),(0,0)) for s in ex.segments}
        bestkey=None; bestids=None; cnt=0
        for combo in itertools.product(*opts):
            port=[o for o in combo if o is not None]
            rel=[sum(o.rel[t] for o in port) for t in range(n)]
            rec=[sum(o.rec[t] for o in port) for t in range(n)]
            if any(rel[t]>ce[t][0] or rec[t]>ce[t][1] for t in range(n)): 
continue
            sf=sum(tg[t][1]*max(0,tg[t][0]-rel[t]) for t in range(n))
            segrel={s:sum(o.trel for o in port if o.segment==s) for s in 
ex.segments}
            df=sum(fl[s][1]*max(0,fl[s][0]-segrel[s]) for s in ex.segments)
            bc=ev['cost_weight']*sum(o.bid for o in 
port)+ev['recovery_weight']*sum(rec)
            ov=sum(max(0,rel[t]-tg[t][0]) for t in range(n))
            key=(sf,df,bc,ov,len(port))
            ids=tuple(sorted(o.oid for o in port))
            if bestkey is None or key<bestkey: bestkey,bestids,cnt=key,ids,1
            elif key==bestkey:
                cnt+=1
                if ids>bestids: bestids=ids
        r=res[(event_id,region)]
        got=tuple(sorted(o.oid for o in r['chosen']))
        checked+=1
        if got!=bestids or r['tied']!=cnt or 
r['candidate_offer_count']!=len(cands):
            mismatch+=1
            if mismatch<4: 
print('MISMATCH',event_id,region,got,bestids,r['tied'],cnt)
        consumed.extend(r['chosen'])
    for o in consumed:
        root=ex.allowance_root.get(o.site_id)
        if root in remaining: remaining[root]-=o.trel
print('checked',checked,'mismatch',mismatch)
EOF

    #!/usr/bin/env python3
    """Build a tiny hand-computed extract in the schema and check derive_report 
on it."""
    import json, os, shutil, subprocess, sys
    
    D = "/tmp/synth/data"
    O = "/tmp/synth/out"
    shutil.rmtree("/tmp/synth", ignore_errors=True)
    os.makedirs(D)
    
    def w(name, text):
        with open(os.path.join(D, name), "w") as f:
            f.write(text)
    
    w("program.ini", """# synthetic
    [units]
    wh_per_tick = 10
    millicents_per_cent = 1000
    
    [region_codes]
    N1 = North
    N2 = North
    S1 = South
    
    [segment_codes]
    C = commercial
    P = public
    
    [segment_rate_adders]
    public = 750
    
    [grants]
    claim_cents_per_weight = 40
    minimum_grant_cents = 25
    
    [export]
    waiver_signed = Y
    """)
    
    events = [
        ("EV-A", "2026-01-01T01:00:00+01:00", 0, 0, 1000),
        ("EV-B", "2026-01-01T00:00:00+00:00", 1, 0, 0),
        ("EV-C", "2026-01-02T00:00:00+00:00", 0, 0, 0),
        ("EV-D", "2026-01-03T00:00:00+00:00", 0, 0, 0),
        ("EV-E", "2026-01-04T00:00:00+00:00", 0, 0, 0),
        ("EV-F", "2026-01-05T00:00:00+00:00", 0, 0, 0),
    ]
    w("events.json", json.dumps([
        {"event_id": e, "name": "n", "dispatch_start": s, "interval_count": 2,
         "objective": {"cost_weight": cw, "recovery_weight": rw},
         "uplift_millicents_per_kwh": up} for e, s, cw, rw, up in events], 
indent=1))
    
    # event, site, region_code, segment_code, beneficiary, critical
    sites = [
        ("EV-A", "S001", "N1", "C", "BEN-1", "N"),
        ("EV-A", "S003", "S1", "P", "BEN-3", "Y"),
        ("EV-A", "S004", "ZZ", "C", "BEN-4", "N"),   # unknown region
        ("EV-A", "S005", "N2", "C", "BEN-5", "N"),
        ("EV-B", "S001", "N1", "C", "BEN-1", "N"),
        ("EV-B", "S002", "N1", "C", "BEN-2", "N"),
        ("EV-C", "S001", "N1", "C", "BEN-1", "N"),
        ("EV-C", "S002", "N1", "C", "BEN-2", "N"),
        ("EV-C", "S006", "N1", "C", "BEN-6", "N"),
        ("EV-D", "S007", "S1", "C", "BEN-7", "N"),
        ("EV-D", "S008", "S1", "C", "BEN-8", "N"),
        ("EV-D", "S009", "S1", "C", "BEN-9", "N"),
        ("EV-E", "S007", "S1", "C", "BEN-7", "N"),
        ("EV-E", "S008", "S1", "C", "BEN-8", "N"),
        ("EV-F", "S007", "S1", "C", "BEN-7", "N"),
        ("EV-F", "S008", "S1", "C", "BEN-8", "N"),
        ("EV-F", "S009", "S1", "C", "BEN-9", "N"),
    ]
    w("sites.csv", 
"event_id,site_id,region_code,segment_code,beneficiary_id,critical\n"
      + "".join(",".join(r) + "\n" for r in sites))
    
    # (event, site): (auth, hold, cool)
    state = {("EV-A", "S005"): (False, False, False),
             ("EV-C", "S002"): (True, True, False),
             ("EV-C", "S006"): (True, False, True)}
    w("site_state.jsonl", "".join(json.dumps({
        "event_id": e, "site_id": s,
        "dispatch_authorized": state.get((e, s), (True, False, False))[0],
        "safety_hold": state.get((e, s), (True, False, False))[1],
        "cooldown": state.get((e, s), (True, False, False))[2]}) + "\n"
        for e, s, *_ in sites))
    
    # (event, site): (cert_pending, status, signed)
    waiv = {("EV-A", "S003"): ("Y", "VALID", "Y"),
            ("EV-A", "S004"): ("N", "NONE", ""),
            ("EV-A", "S005"): ("N", "NONE", ""),
            ("EV-B", "S001"): ("Y", "VALID", "Y"),   # commercial -> only 
CERTIFICATE_PENDING
            ("EV-C", "S001"): ("N", "VALID", ""),
            ("EV-C", "S002"): ("Y", "VALID", "Y")}
    w("continuity_waivers.csv",
      "event_id,site_id,routine_certificate_pending,waiver_status,waiver_signed\
n"
      + "".join("%s,%s,%s,%s,%s\n" % ((e, s) + waiv.get((e, s), ("N", "NONE", 
"N")))
                for e, s, *_ in sites))
    
    # (event, site): (base, override or None, cap)
    cards = {("EV-A", "S001"): (5000, None, 10000),
             ("EV-A", "S003"): (3000, None, 10000),
             ("EV-B", "S001"): (2000, None, 1500),      # cap binding
             ("EV-B", "S002"): (2500, None, 5000),
             ("EV-C", "S002"): (1100000, None, 1000000)}  # cap binding
    body = []
    for e, s, *_ in sites:
        base, over, cap = cards.get((e, s), (1000, None, 2000))
        mid = "<base>%d</base>" % base + ("<override>%d</override>" % over if 
over else "")
        body.append('  <card event="%s" site="%s">%s<cap>%d</cap></card>' % (e, 
s, mid, cap))
    w("rate_cards.xml", '<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8"?>\n<!--\n'
      '  The rate of a site row starts from the base element, or the override 
when present.\n'
      '  credit_cents is the sum over intervals of credited relief ticks times 
wh_per_tick\n'
      '  times the effective rate, divided by 1000 times millicents_per_cent, 
half to even.\n'
      '-->\n<rate_cards>\n' + "\n".join(body) + "\n</rate_cards>\n")
    
    offers = [
        ("OA1", "EV-A", "S001", 100), ("OA2", "EV-A", "S001", 200),
        ("OA4", "EV-A", "S003", 100), ("OA5", "EV-A", "S005", 100),

        ("OC1", "EV-C", "S001", 60), ("OC2", "EV-C", "S002", 60),
        ("OC3", "EV-C", "S006", 60),
    ]
    w("offers.csv", "offer_id,event_id,site_id,mode_label,bid_cost_cents\n"
      + "".join("%s,%s,%s,M,%d\n" % (o, e, s, b) for o, e, s, b in offers))
    
    prof = [
        ("OA1", 1, 1000, 0, 1), ("OA1", 2, 0, 500, 1),
        ("OA2", 1, 500, 0, 2), ("OA2", 2, 0, 250, 2),
        ("OA4", 1, 1500, 0, 1), ("OA4", 2, 1005, 500, 1),

        ("OX1", 1, 1000, 0, 1),
        ("OZZ", 1, 1000, 0, 1),          # orphan profile row
        ("OA1", 3, 1000, 0, 1),          # out of range
        ("OA1", 1, 9999, 9999, 1),       # duplicate
        ("OB1", 1, 1000, 0, 1), ("OB2", 1, 1000, 0, 1),
        ("OC1", 1, 1000, 0, 1), ("OC2", 1, 1000, 0, 1), ("OC3", 1, 3000, 0, 1),
    ]
    w("profiles.tsv", 
"offer_id\tinterval\trelief_wh\trecovery_wh\tregister_multiplier\n"
      + "".join("%s\t%d\t%d\t%d\t%d\n" % r for r in prof))
    
    targets = {("EV-A", "North", 1): (1000, 1), ("EV-A", "South", 1): (500, 1),
               ("EV-B", "North", 1): (1000, 1), ("EV-C", "North", 1): (2000, 1)}
    lines = []
    for e, *_ in events:
        for reg, code in (("North", "N2"), ("South", "S1")):
            for t in (1, 2):
                twh, sw = targets.get((e, reg, t), (0, 1))
                lines.append("%s\t%s\t%d\t%d\t%d\n" % (e, code, t, twh, sw))
    w("targets.tsv", 
"event_id\tregion_code\tinterval\ttarget_wh\tshortfall_weight\n" + 
"".join(lines))
    
    ceil = {("EV-C", "North", 1): (1500, 100000)}
    lines = ["# feeder ceilings\n", "# event_id  code itv relief_wh 
recovery_wh\n"]
    for e, *_ in events:
        for reg, code in (("North", "N1"), ("South", "S1")):
            for t in (1, 2):
                rc, qc = ceil.get((e, reg, t), (100000, 100000))
                lines.append("%-10s%-4s%3d%10d%10d\n" % (e, code, t, rc, qc))
    w("feeder_limits.txt", "".join(lines))
    
    w("segment_floors.json", json.dumps({"floors": [
        {"event_id": "EV-A", "region_code": "N1", "segment_code": "C",
         "floor_wh": 2000, "priority_weight": 5},
        {"event_id": "EV-A", "region_code": "N2", "segment_code": "P",
         "floor_wh": 0, "priority_weight": 1},
        {"event_id": "EV-B", "region_code": "N1", "segment_code": "C",
         "floor_wh": 0, "priority_weight": 0},
    ]}, indent=1))
    
    rw = {("EV-A", "S001"): 3, ("EV-A", "S003"): 2, ("EV-A", "S004"): 5, 
("EV-A", "S005"): 0,
          ("EV-B", "S001"): 1, ("EV-B", "S002"): 0, ("EV-C", "S001"): 7, 
("EV-C", "S002"): 5,
          ("EV-D", "S007"): 1, ("EV-D", "S008"): 1, ("EV-D", "S009"): 1,
          ("EV-E", "S007"): 9, ("EV-E", "S008"): 1,
          ("EV-F", "S007"): 1, ("EV-F", "S008"): 1, ("EV-F", "S009"): 1}
    w("recipient_weights.tsv", "event_id\tsite_id\tgrant_weight\n"
      + "".join("%s\t%s\t%d\n" % (k[0], k[1], v) for k, v in rw.items()))
    
    pools = {("EV-A", "North"): 200, ("EV-B", "North"): 1000, ("EV-B", "South"):
100,
             ("EV-C", "North"): 50, ("EV-D", "South"): 100, ("EV-E", "South"): 
100,
             ("EV-F", "South"): 30}
    code_of = {"North": "N1", "South": "S1"}
    w("pools.txt", "# grant pools\n# event_id  code  pool_cents\n"
      + "".join("%-10s%-4s%12d\n" % (e, code_of[r], c) for (e, r), c in 
pools.items()))
    
    allow = [("S001", 350, ""), ("S002", 0, "S001"), ("S003", 1000, ""), 
("S004", 1000, ""),
             ("S005", 1000, ""), ("S006", 0, "S002"), ("S007", 1000, ""),

import json, os, re
names=['summary.json','policy_audit.json','data_quality.json','region_North.json
',
 'region_South.json','region_West.json','region_East.json',
 'segment_commercial.json','segment_public.json']
for n in names:
    raw=open(n).read()
    assert raw.endswith('\n') and not raw.endswith('\n\n'), n
    doc=json.loads(raw)
    assert isinstance(doc, dict), n
    # sorted keys at every level + integers only
    def walk(o, path):
        if isinstance(o, dict):
            ks=list(o.keys())
            assert ks==sorted(ks), (n, path, ks[:5])
            for k,v in o.items(): walk(v, path+'/'+k)
        elif isinstance(o, list):
            for i,v in enumerate(o): walk(v, path+'/%d'%i)
        else:
            assert isinstance(o,(int,str)) and not isinstance(o,bool), 
(n,path,type(o),o)
    walk(doc,'')
    # re-dump check: file text equals canonical sorted dump
    assert raw == json.dumps(doc, sort_keys=True, indent=1)+'\n', n
    print(n,'ok', len(raw))
# array orders
for r in ['North','South','West','East']:
    d=json.load(open('region_%s.json'%r))
    ids=[e['event_id'] for e in d['events']]
    assert ids==sorted(ids)
    for e in d['events']:
        assert [i['interval'] for i in e['intervals']]==sorted(i['interval'] for
i in e['intervals'])
        assert e['selected_offer_ids']==sorted(e['selected_offer_ids'])
        assert [g['beneficiary_id'] for g in 
e['grants']]==sorted(g['beneficiary_id'] for g in e['grants'])

set(e['compliance_counts'])=={'SAFETY_HOLD','COOLDOWN','CONTINUITY_WAIVED','CERT
IFICATE_PENDING','CLEAR'}

    #!/usr/bin/env python3
    """Worst-case extract at the section 3 limits: 8 sites x 3 candidate offers 
everywhere."""
    import json, os, random, shutil, subprocess, sys, time
    
    D = "/tmp/stress/data"
    O = "/tmp/stress/out"
    shutil.rmtree("/tmp/stress", ignore_errors=True)
    os.makedirs(D)
    rnd = random.Random(7)
    NEV, NSITE, NOFF, NINT = 500, 8, 3, 6
    REGIONS = ["North", "South", "West", "East"]
    CODE = {"North": "N1", "South": "S1", "West": "W1", "East": "E1"}
    
    
    def w(n, t):
        open(os.path.join(D, n), "w").write(t)
    
    
    w("program.ini", """[units]
    wh_per_tick = 10
    millicents_per_cent = 1000
    
    [region_codes]
    N1 = North
    S1 = South
    W1 = West
    E1 = East
    
    [segment_codes]
    C = commercial
    P = public
    
    [segment_rate_adders]
    public = 750
    
    [grants]
    claim_cents_per_weight = 40
    minimum_grant_cents = 25
    """)
    
    events = ["EV-%04d" % i for i in range(NEV)]
    w("events.json", json.dumps([
        {"event_id": e, "name": "n",
         "dispatch_start": "2026-01-%02dT00:00:00+00:00" % (i % 28 + 1),
         "interval_count": NINT,
         "objective": {"cost_weight": 0, "recovery_weight": 0},
         "uplift_millicents_per_kwh": 1000} for i, e in enumerate(events)]))
    
    site_rows, offers, profiles, state, waiv, cards, weights = [], [], [], [], 
[], [], []
    for e in events:
        for r in REGIONS:
            for k in range(NSITE):
                sid = "S%s%s%d" % (e[-4:], r[0], k)
                seg = "C" if k % 2 else "P"
                site_rows.append((e, sid, CODE[r], seg, "BEN-%d" % (k % 3), 
"N"))
                state.append((e, sid))
                waiv.append((e, sid))
                cards.append((e, sid))
                weights.append((e, sid, k % 4))
                for m in range(NOFF):
                    oid = "%s-%s-%d" % (e, sid, m)
                    offers.append((oid, e, sid, rnd.randrange(1000, 9999)))
                    for t in range(1, NINT + 1):
                        profiles.append((oid, t, rnd.randrange(0, 3000) * 10,
                                         rnd.randrange(0, 1000) * 10, 1))
    
    w("sites.csv", 
"event_id,site_id,region_code,segment_code,beneficiary_id,critical\n"
      + "".join(",".join(map(str, r)) + "\n" for r in site_rows))
    w("site_state.jsonl", "".join(json.dumps(
        {"event_id": e, "site_id": s, "dispatch_authorized": True,
         "safety_hold": False, "cooldown": False}) + "\n" for e, s in state))
    w("continuity_waivers.csv",
      "event_id,site_id,routine_certificate_pending,waiver_status,waiver_signed\
n"
      + "".join("%s,%s,N,NONE,N\n" % (e, s) for e, s in waiv))
    w("rate_cards.xml", '<?xml version="1.0"?>\n<rate_cards>\n' + "".join(
        '<card event="%s" site="%s"><base>5000</base><cap>9000</cap></card>\n' %
c
        for c in cards) + "</rate_cards>\n")
    w("offers.csv", "offer_id,event_id,site_id,mode_label,bid_cost_cents\n"
      + "".join("%s,%s,%s,M,%d\n" % o for o in offers))
    w("profiles.tsv", 
"offer_id\tinterval\trelief_wh\trecovery_wh\tregister_multiplier\n"
      + "".join("%s\t%d\t%d\t%d\t%d\n" % p for p in profiles))
    w("targets.tsv", 
"event_id\tregion_code\tinterval\ttarget_wh\tshortfall_weight\n"
      + "".join("%s\t%s\t%d\t%d\t1\n" % (e, CODE[r], t, rnd.randrange(0, 50) * 
1000)
                for e in events for r in REGIONS for t in range(1, NINT + 1)))
    w("feeder_limits.txt", "# ceilings\n" + "".join(
        "%-10s%-4s%3d%10d%10d\n" % (e, CODE[r], t, 10 ** 9, 10 ** 9)
        for e in events for r in REGIONS for t in range(1, NINT + 1)))
    w("segment_floors.json", json.dumps({"floors": [
        {"event_id": e, "region_code": CODE[r], "segment_code": sc,
         "floor_wh": rnd.randrange(0, 200) * 1000, "priority_weight": 
rnd.randrange(0, 6)}
        for e in events for r in REGIONS for sc in ("C", "P")]}))
    w("recipient_weights.tsv", "event_id\tsite_id\tgrant_weight\n"
      + "".join("%s\t%s\t%d\n" % x for x in weights))
    w("pools.txt", "# pools\n" + "".join(
        "%-10s%-4s%12d\n" % (e, CODE[r], rnd.randrange(0, 200000))
        for e in events for r in REGIONS))
    sids = sorted({s for _e, s, *_ in site_rows})
    w("site_allowances.tsv", "# 
allowances\nsite_id\tallowance_ticks\tdraws_from\n"
      + "".join("%s\t%d\t\n" % (s, 10 ** 9) for s in sids))
    w("export_manifest.tsv", "# receipt\nfile\trecords\tfield\tfield_sum\n")
    
    t0 = time.time()
    subprocess.run([sys.executable, "/app/out/derive_report.py", D, O], 
check=True)
    print("elapsed %.1fs" % (time.time() - t0))
    

  kind: "FileEditorAction"

reasoning 0 (total 48.59K) • ↓ output 2.3K (total 94.49K) • $ (total 3.9366)

Reasoning:
292 seconds in the worst case exceeds my 120s budget, so I need to optimize 
further. The objective isn't separable since relief per interval couples the 
sites together, and I can't prune suboptimal branches since I need exact tie 
counts at the optimal value, so a straightforward branch-and-bound won't cut it 
here.

That said, lower-bound pruning could still help when a subtree is strictly worse
than the current best, though computing that bound on a lexicographic 5-tuple is
nontrivial. Meet-in-the-middle also fails since the objective isn't additive 
across site halves due to the nonlinear max(0, target - relief) term, and with 
65536 leaves times 2000 evaluations, I'm looking at over 100 seconds just for 
leaf computation unless I find a cheaper inner loop. I should profile to see 
where the actual time goes, and consider computing the objective incrementally 
as offers are chosen.

Instead of recalculating the full shortfall/overdelivery sums at each leaf, I 
can track deltas as offers are added at each node, updating shortfall and 
overdelivery contributions incrementally rather than recomputing from scratch — 
this shifts the per-target update work (6 iterations) to internal nodes instead 
of leaves, and since the number of internal "add" operations is roughly 
comparable to the leaf count, it should let each update happen once per node 
rather than redundantly at every leaf.

But even minimal leaf work at 131M iterations is still too slow in Python — 
maybe 60-100 seconds just for tuple construction and comparison. I'm wondering 
if I can skip visiting every leaf altogether, but that doesn't seem to reduce 
the fundamental combinatorial count, so let me reconsider the problem's actual 
constraints: at most 8 sites per event-region, each with up to 3 candidate 
offers.

Realistically graders probably won't hit the absolute worst case, but I should 
still optimize to handle it safely within 120 seconds. I'll restructure the DFS 
to maintain the comparison key incrementally rather than rebuilding it at each 
leaf, tracking shortfall, overdelivery, deficit, bid, time-received, and count 
as running values that update on each add.

I'm estimating whether tie-only sorting is already minimal, then checking if 
flattening the recursion into an iterative loop would help—at depth 8 with a 
4-ary branching factor there are roughly 87k nodes per event-region, so 87k 
Python function calls aren't the bottleneck on their own, but with 65536 leaves 
per region the leaf-processing work is likely the dominant cost.

Instead of recursing into leaf calls, I could unroll the final level and loop 
over the four options inline, cutting function-call overhead by 4x while still 
doing the same per-leaf feasibility and tie-break checks. That brings total leaf
evaluations across all event-regions to around 131 million, so I need to 
estimate whether each inline check (interval ops, comparisons, tuple 
construction) keeps total runtime within an acceptable bound.

That still ends up at hundreds of seconds, which is too slow. The real fix has 
to come from pruning the search itself through branch and bound: I need a lower 
bound on the weighted shortfall for any subtree, computed by assuming each 
remaining site contributes its maximum possible relief per interval, so I can 
cut off branches that can't beat the current best key.

This bound alone only lets me prune on the first component of the lexicographic 
tuple reliably—if the lower bound ties the best, I'd need a second bound on the 
deficit component too, but combining both bounds safely requires them to be 
simultaneously achievable, which complicates things.

Checking the other components too: overdelivery lower bound grows since relief 
only increases over time, and count's lower bound is just the current count. 
Even though component 1 uses a maximum future bound while component 4 uses a 
minimum, each is still individually valid, so I need to verify by induction that
if the lower-bound tuple exceeds the best tuple lexicographically, the true 
completion's tuple must also exceed it.

That induction holds: at the first differing index, since all earlier components
match the bound exactly and later true values can only be equal or larger, the 
true tuple is lexicographically worse — confirming the pruning is sound. Now I'm
thinking about how effective this bound will be in practice, especially in the 
stress case where ceilings are infinite and cost/recovery weights are zero, 
leaving shortfall as the dominant comparison across many random targets.

With relief capacity of up to 3000 ticks per interval across six intervals and 
eight sites, the maximum achievable relief vastly exceeds typical shortfall 
targets, so shortfall collapses to zero quickly and matches the best bound in 
most subtrees — same for the deficit term. That leaves overdelivery as the real 
discriminator, and since it grows monotonically as more relief gets added, 
pruning kicks in hard once a branch's overdelivery exceeds the best found, 
though the DFS ordering (favoring "None" options first) means we tend to explore
low-overdelivery portfolios early, which should help pruning efficiency.

The empty portfolio is the first leaf explored, giving an initial bound with 
maximal shortfall but zero overdelivery, so early on there's little pruning on 
the shortfall term until a better solution is found and the bound tightens. I 
think the right move is to just implement this and test it against the stress 
case to see how the bound quality actually performs, and maybe also try 
reordering sites/options to find good solutions faster, since tie-breaking stays
deterministic regardless of exploration order.

Now I'm computing, for each site, the maximum relative value achievable across 
its options for each target, then building suffix sums of those maxima so I can 
bound the best possible shortfall remaining from any point in the search.

Then I combine this with a segment-level deficit bound using similar suffix 
maxima, and prune any branch whose lower-bound key already exceeds the best 
solution found so far. This costs a bit extra per node but should pay off if 
pruning is effective — though I'm now second-guessing whether this complexity is
actually worth it given the grading likely uses extracts similar to the shipped 
example, and the slow 292s case is probably a pathological outlier.

        segrel = [0] * nseg
        chosen = []
        short = sum(weight[t] * target[t] for t in range(n))
        over = 0
        deficit = sum(pw * f for _s, f, pw in floors)
        blend = 0
        best_key = None
        best_ids = None
        best_count = 0
    
        def dfs(i):
            nonlocal short, over, deficit, blend, best_key, best_ids, best_count
            if i == depth:
                key = (short, deficit, blend, over, len(chosen))
                if best_key is None or key < best_key:
                    best_key = key
                    best_ids = tuple(sorted(o.oid for o in chosen))
                    best_count = 1
                elif key == best_key:
                    best_count += 1
                    ids = tuple(sorted(o.oid for o in chosen))
                    if ids > best_ids:
                        best_ids = ids
                return
            if best_key is not None:
                lb_short = 0
                sr = suf_rel[i]
                for t in range(n):
                    d = target[t] - rel[t] - sr[t]
                    if d > 0:
                        lb_short += weight[t] * d
                lb_deficit = 0
                ss = suf_seg[i]
                for k in range(nseg):
                    d = floors[k][1] - segrel[k] - ss[k]
                    if d > 0:
                        lb_deficit += floors[k][2] * d
                if (lb_short, lb_deficit, blend, over, len(chosen)) > best_key:
                    return
            for o in options[i]:
                if o is None:
                    dfs(i + 1)
                    continue
                orel, orec = o.rel, o.rec
                feasible = True
                for t in range(n):
                    if rel[t] + orel[t] > rel_ceil[t] or rec[t] + orec[t] > 
rec_ceil[t]:
                        feasible = False
                        break
                if not feasible:
                    continue
                short0, over0, deficit0, blend0 = short, over, deficit, blend
                for t in range(n):
                    a = orel[t]
                    if a:
                        old = rel[t]
                        new = old + a
                        rel[t] = new
                        tg = target[t]
                        if old < tg:
                            short -= weight[t] * ((tg - old) - (tg - new if new 
< tg else 0))
                        if new > tg:
                            over += new - tg - (old - tg if old > tg else 0)
                    b = orec[t]
                    if b:
                        rec[t] += b
                si = seg_index.get(o.segment)
                if si is not None:
                    old = segrel[si]
                    new = old + o.trel
                    segrel[si] = new
                    f, pw = floors[si][1], floors[si][2]
                    if old < f:
                        deficit -= pw * ((f - old) - (f - new if new < f else 
0))
                blend += cost_weight * o.bid + recovery_weight * o.trec
                chosen.append(o)
                dfs(i + 1)
                chosen.pop()
                blend, deficit, over, short = blend0, deficit0, over0, short0
                if si is not None:
                    segrel[si] -= o.trel
                for t in range(n):
                    rel[t] -= orel[t]
                    rec[t] -= orec[t]
    
        dfs(0)
        by_id = {o.oid: o for opts in options for o in opts if o is not None}
        return [by_id[i] for i in best_ids], best_count

Summary: Stress with unreachable targets and floors

Reasoning:
The runtime dropped from 292s to 12.8s with identical output. Now I want to 
stress-test this with an adversarial case: setting shortfall, deficit, targets, 
and floors all to zero, and zeroing out cost and recovery weights, so that three
of the five scoring components collapse to zero for every portfolio, leaving 
only overdelivery and count to decide the winner—which should then trivially 
become the empty portfolio.

I also want to try the opposite extreme: targets so large they're unreachable, 
forcing shortfall to always dominate and making minimizing shortfall equivalent 
to maximizing total relief, which gives a bound that's nearly achievable per 
interval and should stress whether pruning stays tight.

The other adversarial case is full ties — identical offers at every site 
creating huge groups of portfolios with the same count and relief that are 
indistinguishable by the bound and must all be visited, like choosing 4 of 8 
sites with 3 identical offers each producing thousands of tied combinations with
no way to prune them.

I want to actually test the unreachable-targets scenario now by building a 
stress generator with targets set so high they can't possibly be met, random 
weights, large floors, and zero cost/recovery weighting, then parameterize it 
through an environment variable to avoid rewriting the script from scratch.

    #!/usr/bin/env python3
    """Pathological tie extract: 8 interchangeable sites x 3 identical offers, 
ceiling of 6."""
    import json, os, shutil, subprocess, sys, time
    
    D, O = "/tmp/ties/data", "/tmp/ties/out"
    shutil.rmtree("/tmp/ties", ignore_errors=True)
    os.makedirs(D)
    NEV, NSITE, NOFF, NINT = 500, 8, 3, 6
    REGIONS = ["North", "South", "West", "East"]
    CODE = {"North": "N1", "South": "S1", "West": "W1", "East": "E1"}
    
    
    def w(n, t):
        open(os.path.join(D, n), "w").write(t)
    
    
    w("program.ini", "[units]\nwh_per_tick = 10\nmillicents_per_cent = 1000\n\n"
      "[region_codes]\nN1 = North\nS1 = South\nW1 = West\nE1 = East\n\n"
      "[segment_codes]\nC = commercial\nP = public\n\n"
      "[segment_rate_adders]\npublic = 750\n\n"
      "[grants]\nclaim_cents_per_weight = 40\nminimum_grant_cents = 25\n")
    
    events = ["EV-%04d" % i for i in range(NEV)]
    w("events.json", json.dumps([
        {"event_id": e, "name": "n", "dispatch_start": 
"2026-01-01T00:00:00+00:00",
         "interval_count": NINT, "objective": {"cost_weight": 0, 
"recovery_weight": 0},
         "uplift_millicents_per_kwh": 1000} for e in events]))
    
    rows, offs, prof = [], [], []
    for e in events:
        for r in REGIONS:
            for k in range(NSITE):
                sid = "S%s%s%d" % (e[-4:], r[0], k)
                rows.append((e, sid))
                for m in range(NOFF):
                    oid = "%s-%s-%d" % (e, sid, m)
                    offs.append((oid, e, sid, 500))
                    for t in range(1, NINT + 1):
                        prof.append((oid, t, 1000, 0, 1))   # 100 ticks every 
interval
    
    w("sites.csv", 
"event_id,site_id,region_code,segment_code,beneficiary_id,critical\n"
      + "".join("%s,%s,%s,C,BEN-1,N\n" % (e, s, CODE[[r for r in REGIONS if r[0]
== s[5]][0]])
                for e, s in rows))
    w("site_state.jsonl", "".join(json.dumps(
        {"event_id": e, "site_id": s, "dispatch_authorized": True,
         "safety_hold": False, "cooldown": False}) + "\n" for e, s in rows))
    w("continuity_waivers.csv",
      "event_id,site_id,routine_certificate_pending,waiver_status,waiver_signed\
n"
      + "".join("%s,%s,N,NONE,N\n" % r for r in rows))
    w("rate_cards.xml", '<?xml version="1.0"?>\n<rate_cards>\n' + "".join(
        '<card event="%s" site="%s"><base>5000</base><cap>9000</cap></card>\n' %
r
        for r in rows) + "</rate_cards>\n")
    w("offers.csv", "offer_id,event_id,site_id,mode_label,bid_cost_cents\n"
      + "".join("%s,%s,%s,M,%d\n" % o for o in offs))
    w("profiles.tsv", 
"offer_id\tinterval\trelief_wh\trecovery_wh\tregister_multiplier\n"
      + "".join("%s\t%d\t%d\t%d\t%d\n" % p for p in prof))
    w("targets.tsv", 
"event_id\tregion_code\tinterval\ttarget_wh\tshortfall_weight\n"
      + "".join("%s\t%s\t%d\t6000\t1\n" % (e, CODE[r], t)
                for e in events for r in REGIONS for t in range(1, NINT + 1)))
    w("feeder_limits.txt", "# ceilings\n" + "".join(
        "%-10s%-4s%3d%10d%10d\n" % (e, CODE[r], t, 6000, 10 ** 9)
        for e in events for r in REGIONS for t in range(1, NINT + 1)))
    w("segment_floors.json", json.dumps({"floors": []}))
    w("recipient_weights.tsv", "event_id\tsite_id\tgrant_weight\n")
    w("pools.txt", "# pools\n")
    w("site_allowances.tsv", "# 
allowances\nsite_id\tallowance_ticks\tdraws_from\n"
      + "".join("%s\t%d\t\n" % (s, 10 ** 9) for s in sorted({s for _e, s in 
rows})))
    w("export_manifest.tsv", "# receipt\nfile\trecords\tfield\tfield_sum\n")
    
    t0 = time.time()
    subprocess.run([sys.executable, "/app/out/derive_report.py", D, O], 
check=True)
    print("elapsed %.1fs" % (time.time() - t0))
    p = json.load(open(os.path.join(O, "policy_audit.json")))
    print("tie entries", len(p["tie_decisions"]), p["tie_decisions"][0])

The result confirms my prediction of 20412 tied portfolios, correctly selecting 
the lexicographically greatest tuple. But runtime hit 140s, just over the 120s 
limit—this is a highly contrived edge case where every one of 2000 event-regions
is maximally degenerate, so I need to optimize the tie-handling path since it's 
clearly the bottleneck.

Rather than sorting offer id generators at every tied leaf, I should maintain a 
parallel list of ids alongside the chosen offers to avoid the generator 
overhead, and think about whether I can compare tied portfolios lazily instead 
of eagerly sorting every time.

Since sites are distinct, only the multiset of ids matters, but sorting six 
short strings across 41 million comparisons is clearly what's driving the 140s 
runtime. I need a way to find the max sorted-id tuple among tied portfolios 
without sorting each one fully — maybe by pre-filtering on a cheap signal like 
the minimum id before doing a full comparison, or restructuring the search so 
ties resolve through traversal order rather than explicit tuple comparison.

Simplest fix is swapping to list comparisons instead of generator-based tuple 
building, which might buy 2-3x. But even then, getting from 3.4µs to 2.5µs per 
leaf is a tight margin, and I'm questioning whether this tied edge case is worth
the extra engineering effort versus just accepting the runtime.

The spec's constraint (max 8 sites, 3 offers each, 120s budget) reads like a 
soft promise about realistic inputs, and the actual sample extracts are nowhere 
near this degenerate scale. Still, worth grabbing easy wins: maintaining a 
parallel chosen_ids list for sorted comparisons and caching len(chosen) as a 
running counter to avoid recomputation.

I'm also wondering if there's a smarter tie-break than full sorted-tuple 
comparison — maybe maintaining a sorted structure incrementally during the DFS 
rather than rebuilding it each time, though nothing concrete comes to mind yet.

Rather than sorting at every leaf, I can maintain a running sorted list with 
bisect.insort and remove on backtrack, so the leaf only needs a tuple build, a 
comparison, and a list comparison against best — cutting per-leaf cost 
dramatically, maybe down to roughly 60 seconds total.

Tracking count via len(ids) is fine since list length is O(1). At each leaf I 
compare the key tuple against the best found so far, updating best_ids with a 
copy when strictly better, and when tied, comparing the id lists 
lexicographically to pick the greater one as tie-break, matching the spec's rule
for comparing sets of identifiers.

I'm weighing whether to avoid building the full tuple each time for speed, but 
the overhead is tiny, so I'll keep the tuple-based comparison and just focus on 
verifying the implementation with real measurements.

Summary: Profile tie-heavy case on smaller input

Reasoning:
The improvement is marginal, only 140 to 124 seconds, so the real bottleneck 
must be the DFS traversal itself rather than sorting. In this degenerate case 
where all 3 offers per site are identical across 8 sites, the search is 
exploring a huge combinatorial space, roughly summing binomial coefficients 
times powers of 3 up to the ceiling of 6 offers, which explains the blowup.

Working through the arithmetic, that's around 41,479 leaves per event-region, 
multiplied by 2000 events giving roughly 83 million leaves total, which at 124 
seconds comes out to about 1.5 microseconds per leaf — already quite tight for 
Python's overhead.

I'm examining whether the pruning bound can cut branches smaller than size 6: 
since shortfall only hits zero once six sites are chosen, the lower bound only 
prunes subtrees where the count of chosen plus remaining sites falls below 6, 
which unfortunately leaves most of the tree still reachable and explains why so 
many leaves must be visited.

At i=7 with cnt=4, cnt+1=5<6 means the bound prunes it, so only cnt=5 or higher 
survives to the leaf level. That brings the visited count down to roughly 40M 
nodes, around 124 seconds total.

To speed things up further, I could try reordering so the "None" branch is 
explored last rather than first, which would make the first found leaf 
correspond to the all-offers solution.

Trying all-offers-first means checking infeasible portfolios early, but it 
should still improve pruning since finding high-relief, low-shortfall solutions 
sooner tightens bounds faster, and reordering doesn't affect correctness since 
the max is taken over all branches regardless of order. I want to test putting 
the "None" option last to see if it helps, and also consider inlining the final 
recursion level to cut function call overhead. Let me try the reordering first.

Though stepping back, I'm questioning whether optimizing for the 124s maximally 
degenerate benchmark is even worthwhile given realistic extracts run in under a 
second — but in the tied-portfolio case, we're forced to enumerate all 20412 
tied combinations per event-region regardless of ordering, giving roughly 41M 
leaves as a hard floor around 60s, so the best case from reordering is maybe 
halving the non-tied-case runtime. I'll test the None-last ordering on a smaller
event count first to iterate quickly before committing to a change.

For optimization, I'm considering specializing the nseg==0 case to skip segment 
lookups, merging the feasibility check and update into a single pass with 
rollback to avoid a redundant six-iteration loop, and precomputing which 
intervals have non-zero rel/rec values to skip zeros — though in this test all 
six intervals are non-zero, so that last idea wouldn't help here.

I'm also weighing whether the bound computation at the deepest internal level is
worth skipping, since evaluating children directly there is nearly as cheap as 
computing the bound, but stepping back, this benchmark is a deliberately 
pathological case I constructed myself — real shipped data runs in 0.7s, and 
other stress tests take 12.8s and 23.2s, so I should focus on the cheap wins 
rather than over-optimizing for this edge case.

Given that, I'll implement two optimizations: precomputing a per-site segment 
index as a list lookup instead of a dict lookup to avoid per-node overhead when 
there are no segments, and merging the feasibility check into the update loop 
with partial rollback. Writing the segment indexing logic...

Still writing rollback logic...

I'm also planning to hoist frequently used closure variables like weight, 
target, and the ceilings into locals, and replace repeated len(ids) calls with a
maintained counter to cut overhead. I want to skip the deficit loop entirely 
when there are no segments, and restructure the bound computation to 
short-circuit more cases.

I'll rewrite the whole solve function body from scratch with these optimizations
baked in, rather than patching pieces incrementally.

   351      ev = ex.events[event_id]
   352      n = ev["interval_count"]
   353      target, weight, rel_ceil, rec_ceil = [], [], [], []
   354      for t in range(1, n + 1):
   355          tg, w = ex.targets[(event_id, region, t)]
   356          target.append(tg)
   357          weight.append(w)
   358          rc, qc = ex.ceilings[(event_id, region, t)]
   359          rel_ceil.append(rc)
   360          rec_ceil.append(qc)
   361      floors = []
   362      for seg in ex.segments:

   364          if f > 0 and pw > 0:
   365              floors.append((seg, f, pw))
   366      seg_index = {seg: i for i, (seg, _f, _w) in enumerate(floors)}
   367      nseg = len(floors)

   375      # Per-component optimistic bounds for the sites from index i 
onwards: the most

        options = [[None] + sorted(site_options[s], key=lambda o: o.oid)

        # Per-component optimistic bounds for the sites from index i onwards: 
the most

        suf_rel = [[0] * n for _ in range(depth + 1)]
        suf_seg = [[0] * nseg for _ in range(depth + 1)]
        for i in range(depth - 1, -1, -1):
            offs = options[i][1:]

        rel = [0] * n

        segrel = [0] * nseg

        short = sum(weight[t] * target[t] for t in range(n))
        over = 0
        deficit = sum(pw * f for _s, f, pw in floors)
        blend = 0
        best_key = None
        best_ids = None
        best_count = 0
    
        def dfs(i):
            nonlocal short, over, deficit, blend, best_key, best_ids, best_count
            if i == depth:
                key = (short, deficit, blend, over, len(ids))
                if best_key is None or key < best_key:
                    best_key = key
                    best_ids = ids.copy()
                    best_count = 1
                elif key == best_key:
                    best_count += 1
                    if ids > best_ids:
                        best_ids = ids.copy()
                return
            if best_key is not None:
                lb_short = 0
                sr = suf_rel[i]
                for t in range(n):
                    d = target[t] - rel[t] - sr[t]
                    if d > 0:
                        lb_short += weight[t] * d
                lb_deficit = 0
                ss = suf_seg[i]
                for k in range(nseg):
                    d = floors[k][1] - segrel[k] - ss[k]
                    if d > 0:
                        lb_deficit += floors[k][2] * d
                if (lb_short, lb_deficit, blend, over, len(ids)) > best_key:
                    return
            for o in options[i]:
                if o is None:
                    dfs(i + 1)
                    continue
                orel, orec = o.rel, o.rec
                feasible = True
                for t in range(n):
                    if rel[t] + orel[t] > rel_ceil[t] or rec[t] + orec[t] > 
rec_ceil[t]:
                        feasible = False
                        break
                if not feasible:
                    continue
                short0, over0, deficit0, blend0 = short, over, deficit, blend
                for t in range(n):
                    a = orel[t]
                    if a:
                        old = rel[t]
                        new = old + a
                        rel[t] = new
                        tg = target[t]
                        if old < tg:
                            short -= weight[t] * ((tg - old) - (tg - new if new 
< tg else 0))
                        if new > tg:
                            over += new - tg - (old - tg if old > tg else 0)
                    b = orec[t]
                    if b:
                        rec[t] += b
                si = seg_index.get(o.segment)
                if si is not None:
                    old = segrel[si]
                    new = old + o.trel
                    segrel[si] = new
                    f, pw = floors[si][1], floors[si][2]
                    if old < f:
                        deficit -= pw * ((f - old) - (f - new if new < f else

                    segrel[si] -= o.trel
                for t in range(n):

                    rec[t] -= orec[t]

        options = [[None] + sorted(site_options[s], key=lambda o: o.oid)
                   for s in sorted(site_options)]
        depth = len(options)
        # All offers of a site belong to one site row, hence to one segment.
        site_seg = [seg_index.get(opts[1].segment) for opts in options]
    
        # Optimistic bounds for the sites from index i onwards: the most relief 
and the
        # most segment relief any completion could still add.
        suf_rel = [[0] * n for _ in range(depth + 1)]
        suf_seg = [[0] * nseg for _ in range(depth + 1)]
        for i in range(depth - 1, -1, -1):
            offs = options[i][1:]
            for t in range(n):
                suf_rel[i][t] = suf_rel[i + 1][t] + max(o.rel[t] for o in offs)
            most = max(o.trel for o in offs)
            for k in range(nseg):
                suf_seg[i][k] = suf_seg[i + 1][k] + (most if k == site_seg[i] 
else 0)
    
        rel = [0] * n
        rec = [0] * n
        segrel = [0] * nseg
        ids = []  # offer ids of the portfolio under construction, kept sorted 
ascending
        short = sum(weight[t] * target[t] for t in range(n))
        over = 0
        deficit = sum(pw * f for _s, f, pw in floors)
        blend = 0
        count = 0
        best_key = None
        best_ids = None
        best_count = 0
    
        def dfs(i):
            nonlocal short, over, deficit, blend, count
            nonlocal best_key, best_ids, best_count
            if i == depth:
                key = (short, deficit, blend, over, count)
                if best_key is None or key < best_key:
                    best_key = key
                    best_ids = ids.copy()
                    best_count = 1
                elif key == best_key:
                    best_count += 1
                    if ids > best_ids:
                        best_ids = ids.copy()
                return
            if best_key is not None:
                lb_short = 0
                sr = suf_rel[i]
                for t in range(n):
                    d = target[t] - rel[t] - sr[t]
                    if d > 0:
                        lb_short += weight[t] * d
                lb_deficit = 0
                if nseg:
                    ss = suf_seg[i]
                    for k in range(nseg):
                        d = floors[k][1] - segrel[k] - ss[k]
                        if d > 0:
                            lb_deficit += floors[k][2] * d
                if (lb_short, lb_deficit, blend, over, count) > best_key:
                    return
            si = site_seg[i]
            for o in options[i]:
                if o is None:
                    dfs(i + 1)
                    continue
                orel, orec = o.rel, o.rec
                short0, over0 = short, over
                bad = -1
                for t in range(n):
                    a = orel[t]
                    b = orec[t]
                    new = rel[t] + a
                    nrec = rec[t] + b
                    if new > rel_ceil[t] or nrec > rec_ceil[t]:
                        bad = t
                        break
                    if a:
                        old = rel[t]
                        rel[t] = new
                        tg = target[t]
                        if old < tg:
                            short -= weight[t] * ((tg - old) - (tg - new if new 
< tg else 0))
                        if new > tg:
                            over += new - tg - (old - tg if old > tg else 0)
                    if b:
                        rec[t] = nrec
                if bad >= 0:
                    for t in range(bad):
                        rel[t] -= orel[t]
                        rec[t] -= orec[t]
                    short, over = short0, over0
                    continue
                deficit0, blend0 = deficit, blend
                if si is not None:
                    old = segrel[si]
                    new = old + o.trel
                    segrel[si] = new
                    f, pw = floors[si][1], floors[si][2]
                    if old < f:
                        deficit -= pw * ((f - old) - (f - new if new < f else 
0))
                blend += cost_weight * o.bid + recovery_weight * o.trec
                count += 1
                pos = bisect_left(ids, o.oid)
                ids.insert(pos, o.oid)
                dfs(i + 1)
                del ids[pos]
                count -= 1
                blend, deficit, over, short = blend0, deficit0, over0, short0
                if si is not None:
                    segrel[si] -= o.trel
                for t in range(n):
                    rel[t] -= orel[t]
                    rec[t] -= orec[t]
    
        dfs(0)
        by_id = {o.oid: o for opts in options for o in opts if o is not None}
        return [by_id[i] for i in best_ids], best_count

  kind: "FileEditorAction"

    17  
    18  
    19  def rhu(num, den):
    20      """num/den rounded half up (num >= 0, den > 0)."""
    21      return (2 * num + den) // (2 * den)
    22  
    23  
    24  def rhe(num, den):
    25      """num/den rounded half to even (num >= 0, den > 0)."""
    26      q, r = divmod(num, den)
    27      two_r = 2 * r
    28      if two_r > den or (two_r == den and q % 2):
    29          q += 1
    30      return q
    31  
    32  
    33  def raw_lines(path):
    34      with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as fh:
    35          lines = fh.read().split("\n")
    36      if lines and lines[-1] == "":
    37          lines.pop()
    38      return lines
    39  
    40  
    41  def content_lines(path):
    42      """Non-comment, non-blank lines of a commented text file."""
    43      return [l for l in raw_lines(path) if l.strip() and not 
l.startswith("#")]
    44  
    45  
    46  def csv_rows(path, delimiter=","):
    47      with open(path, encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
    48          return list(csv.DictReader(fh, delimiter=delimiter))

   115                  "uplift": int(e["uplift_millicents_per_kwh"]),
   116                  "start": datetime.fromisoformat(e["dispatch_start"]),
   117              }
   118          self.event_ids = sorted(self.events)
   119          season = sorted(self.events.values(), key=lambda e: 
e["event_id"], reverse=True)

Reasoning:

mis-parse TSV fields containing quote characters, but since values here are just
ids/integers, it should be acceptable.

The inconsistency between manual split functions and DictReader-based ones is 
minor since identifiers like `W26-1234` won't trigger quoting issues. Given the 
real data output is already byte-identical to the brute-force verified

import sys, itertools, collections
sys.path.insert(0,'out')
import derive_report as d
ex=d.Extract('data'); offers=d.build_offers(ex)
ber=collections.defaultdict(list)
for o in offers: ber[(o.event_id, 
ex.site_rows[(o.event_id,o.site_id)]['region'])].append(o)
res,_=d.run(ex)
remaining={r:ex.allowance_ticks[r] for r in set(ex.allowance_root.values())}
bad=0
for event_id in ex.season_order:
    ev=ex.events[event_id]; n=ev['interval_count']; used=[]
    for region in ex.regions:
        cands=[o for o in ber[(event_id,region)]
               if 
ex.site_rows[(o.event_id,o.site_id)].get('dispatch_authorized')
               and o.trel<=remaining.get(ex.allowance_root.get(o.site_id),0)]
        bysite=collections.defaultdict(list)
        for o in cands: bysite[o.site_id].append(o)
        opts=[[None]+sorted(v,key=lambda o:o.oid) for _k,v in 
sorted(bysite.items())]
        tg=[ex.targets[(event_id,region,t)] for t in range(1,n+1)]
        ce=[ex.ceilings[(event_id,region,t)] for t in range(1,n+1)]
        fl={s:ex.floors.get((event_id,region,s),(0,0)) for s in ex.segments}
        bk=bi=None; cnt=0
        for combo in itertools.product(*opts):
            port=[o for o in combo if o is not None]
            rel=[sum(o.rel[t] for o in port) for t in range(n)]
            rec=[sum(o.rec[t] for o in port) for t in range(n)]
            if any(rel[t]>ce[t][0] or rec[t]>ce[t][1] for t in range(n)): 
continue
            key=(sum(tg[t][1]*max(0,tg[t][0]-rel[t]) for t in range(n)),
                 sum(fl[s][1]*max(0,fl[s][0]-sum(o.trel for o in port if 
o.segment==s)) for s in ex.segments),
                 ev['cost_weight']*sum(o.bid for o in 
port)+ev['recovery_weight']*sum(rec),
                 sum(max(0,rel[t]-tg[t][0]) for t in range(n)), len(port))
            ids=tuple(sorted(o.oid for o in port))
            if bk is None or key<bk: bk,bi,cnt=key,ids,1
            elif key==bk:
                cnt+=1
                if ids>bi: bi=ids
        r=res[(event_id,region)]
        if tuple(sorted(o.oid for o in r['chosen']))!=bi or r['tied']!=cnt or 
r['candidate_offer_count']!=len(cands):
            bad+=1
        used.extend(r['chosen'])
    for o in used:
        rt=ex.allowance_root.get(o.site_id)
        if rt in remaining: remaining[rt]-=o.trel
print('brute-force mismatches:', bad)

    assert raw==json.dumps(doc,sort_keys=True,indent=1)+'\n', n
    def walk(o):
        if isinstance(o,dict):
            assert list(o)==sorted(o)
            for v in o.values(): walk(v)
        elif isinstance(o,list):
            for v in o: walk(v)
        else: assert isinstance(o,(int,str)) and not isinstance(o,bool), (n,o)
    walk(doc)

    # District heat flexibility programme extract
    
    `/app/data/schema.md` is the binding rulebook for a season extract; the 
comment blocks at
    the top of `rate_cards.xml` (rate/credit formation) and 
`site_allowances.tsv` (season order
    and allowance drawing) are equally binding. `/app/out/derive_report.py 
DATA_DIR OUT_DIR`
    turns any extract in that schema into the nine JSON reports.
    
    Points that are easy to get wrong:
    
    - `program.ini` `[export]` carries no rule, so its `waiver_signed = Y` is 
**not** a default
      for empty `waiver_signed` fields in `continuity_waivers.csv`.
    - Season order (allowance drawing and grant carry) is by `dispatch_start` 
instant, ties
      broken by **descending** `event_id` (section 2.4 serves equals in 
descending order).
      This is not `event_id` order.
    - Unallocated grant cents carry into the same region's next event in season 
order, so
      funded pools snowball across the season.
    - Every Wh figure becomes ticks (half up) the moment it is derived from its 
record;
      credits round half to **even**, per interval, before summing.
    - Caps bind after the uplift and the segment rate adder are added.
    - Portfolio choice is a lexicographic 5-component minimisation; remaining 
ties pick the
      greatest tuple of sorted offer ids. `tied_portfolio_count` needs the exact
count of
      portfolios at the best value, so pruning must be provably lossless.
    
    Verification harnesses (regenerate as needed, they live outside the repo):
    `/tmp/mk_synth.py` (hand-computed small extract covering every branch),
    `/tmp/mk_stress.py`, `/tmp/mk_ties.py` (section 3 worst-case size and tie 
load).

tables, the fixed-width `feeder_limits.txt` (cols 
1\u201110/11\u201114/15\u201117/18\u201127/28\u201137) and `pools.txt`

`rate_cards.xml` and `site_allowances.tsv`. Records are keyed on
