and counted, and the state/waiver/card/weight lines for them are dropped. Every 
Wh figure is quantised to ticks (half up) the moment it is derived from its 
record.\n\n**Derivation.** Site rows get one compliance label by the 2.6 
ranking, and an effective rate of `override|base + event uplift + segment 
adder`, capped last (cap-binding = raw > cap). Offer credits are the 
per-interval `credited ticks \u00d7 wh_per_tick \u00d7 rate / (1000 \u00d7

exact.\n\n**Grants.** Funded pool `max(0, pool + carried \u2212 credits)`, then:
no recipients or W=0 \u2192 all unallocated; claims \u2264 F \u2192 claims paid;

id), the `minimum_grant_cents` filter, the second share over the survivors, and 
the all-below-minimum case. Residual cents and residual ties come from the last 
largest-remainder share.\n\nAll JSON is written with `sort_keys`, integers only 
(no floats, no booleans), a trailing newline, and the array orders the contract 
specifies.\n\n## How I checked it\n\n- **Parser against the export receipt.**

shipped extract takes 0.8 s. Output is byte-identical before and after the

Done. `/app/out/derive_report.py` (Python 3.12 stdlib only) is in place and has 
been run on `/app/data`, producing all nine reports in `/app/out` (0.8 s).

## What the program does

**Parsing.** One `Extract` class reads every file the rulebook names: 
`program.ini` via `configparser` (region/segment codes and rate adders matched 
case-insensitively, `[export]` deliberately ignored because the contract says it
carries no rule — so an empty `waiver_signed` is *not* `Y`), `events.json`, the 
CSV/TSV tables, the fixed-width `feeder_limits.txt` (cols 
1‑10/11‑14/15‑17/18‑27/28‑37) and `pools.txt` (1‑10/11‑14/15‑26), 
`site_state.jsonl`, `segment_floors.json`, `rate_cards.xml` and 
`site_allowances.tsv`. Records are keyed on **region/segment names**, never 
codes. Rows with an unknown region are skipped and counted, and the 
state/waiver/card/weight lines for them are dropped. Every Wh figure is 
quantised to ticks (half up) the moment it is derived from its record.

**Derivation.** Site rows get one compliance label by the 2.6 ranking, and an 
effective rate of `override|base + event uplift + segment adder`, capped last 
(cap-binding = raw > cap). Offer credits are the per-interval `credited ticks × 
wh_per_tick × rate / (1000 × millicents_per_cent)` rounded **half to even**, 
then summed.

**Season walk.** Events are processed in `dispatch_start` order with equal 
instants in *descending* `event_id` (section 2.4), which drives both allowance 
drawing (`draws_from` chains resolved to a root allowance; offers whose total 
relief exceeds what remains are not candidates) and the grant carry (a region's 
unallocated cents fund its next event, so pools compound).

**Optimisation.** For each event-region a DFS over at-most-one-offer-per-site 
enumerates feasible portfolios and minimises the lexicographic 5-tuple (weighted
shortfall, weighted floor deficit, blended cost, overdelivery, offer count), 
breaking remaining ties on the greatest tuple of sorted offer ids and counting 
the portfolios at the best value. Components are maintained incrementally; 
subtrees are cut only by component-wise *lower* bounds compared 
lexicographically against the current best, which is provably lossless, so 
`tied_portfolio_count` stays exact.

**Grants.** Funded pool `max(0, pool + carried − credits)`, then: no recipients 
or W=0 → all unallocated; claims ≤ F → claims paid; otherwise largest remainder 
(equal remainders served in descending beneficiary id), the 
`minimum_grant_cents` filter, the second share over the survivors, and the 
all-below-minimum case. Residual cents and residual ties come from the last 
largest-remainder share.

All JSON is written with `sort_keys`, integers only (no floats, no booleans), a 
trailing newline, and the array orders the contract specifies.

## How I checked it

- **Parser against the export receipt.** Every record count and `field_sum` in 
`export_manifest.tsv` (18 014 offers, 108 036 profile rows, 408 400 000 ceiling 
Wh, 209 111 620 pool cents, 93 338 220 allowance ticks, 64 854 000 base 
mill-cents, …) reproduces exactly from my readers, including the two fixed-width
files.
- **Independent brute force of the optimiser.** A separate `itertools.product` 
re-implementation of feasibility, all five components and the tie-break was run 
over all 2 000 event-regions, replaying the allowance state: **0 mismatches** in
chosen portfolio, tie count and candidate count.
- **Hand-computed synthetic extract** (`/tmp/mk_synth.py`, ~70 asserted values) 
exercising every branch the shipped data leaves cold: same-instant season 
ordering, the carry chain, claims-paid vs largest-remainder, residual cents, a 
residual tie, minimum-grant exclusion with a second share, the all-below-minimum
case, weight-0 beneficiaries, `CONTINUITY_WAIVED` double weighting, unknown 
regions, orphan/out-of-range/duplicate profile rows, a two-level `draws_from` 
chain with allowance blocking, cap binding, override cards, segment adders, 
half-up tick rounding (1 005 Wh → 101 ticks) and half-to-even credits (1.5→2 and
2.5→2), portfolio ties, floor deficits and `floor_attainment_bp`, and missing 
floor/pool defaults. All pass.
- **Cross-file consistency.** `summary.json` equals the sums over the four 
region files *and* over the two segment files for every shared total; compliance
counts and selected offer sets agree across region, segment and audit files.
- **Format.** Each of the nine files round-trips byte-for-byte through 
`json.dumps(sort_keys=True)`, contains only ints and strings, and every array is
in the prescribed order.
- **Scale.** Generated extracts at the section 3 ceiling (500 events × 4 regions
× 8 sites × 3 offers): 11.7 s for random profiles, 23.2 s with unreachable 
targets/floors, and 114 s for a deliberately degenerate construction where all 2
000 event-regions have 20 412-way ties — all inside the 120 s budget; the 
shipped extract takes 0.8 s. Output is byte-identical before and after the 
pruning work, and identical under a reversed DFS option order, confirming the 
choice is order-independent.

A short note on a figure that looks surprising but follows the contract: 
`grant_funded_cents` reaches 51 615 561 834 because section 7 carries each 
event-region's *unallocated* cents into the same region's next event in season 
order, and claims (weight × 40 cents) are tiny next to the pools, so the funded 
pool compounds across the 500 events.

I also left `/app/AGENTS.md` with the rulebook pitfalls and pointers to the test
harnesses.
